顧
• AI驅動獲利成長，企業基本面仍具支撐：美國企業獲利維持強勁，背後主要受經濟展望穩健及AI資本支出熱潮支撐。從獲利
來看，市場共識預期標普500企業EPS成長率2026年達28%，2027年及2028年則分別為20%及16%，雖呈現逐步降溫，但
仍維持雙位數增長。其中，AI基礎建設與大型雲端業者仍是重要驅動來源，2026年對EPS成長的貢獻合計約19%，2027、
2028年仍分別貢獻14%及11%。
• 獲利上修降低估值壓力：標普500預期本益比已由約23倍降至19倍，且並非主要來自股價修正，而是企業獲利預期上調所致。
雖然未來EPS成長率若由高檔回落，市場仍可能面臨評價修正壓力，但在企業獲利維持成長、AI投資持續挹注及估值已較高
點收斂的情況下，標普500基本面仍具支撐。因此，短期應留意獲利增速放緩與市場持倉偏高所帶來的波動，但從獲利成長

與估值兩大面向觀察，標普500仍具中長期配置價值。

中國製造業PMI(%) 企業利潤前瞻指數反彈上行(%)
BCI:企業利潤前瞻指數 BCI:企業銷售前瞻指數
2026年製造業PMI 2024年 2025年 2023年
生產回升與價格上漲同步推動中國PMI改善
• 生產回升與價格上漲同步推動 中國PMI改善： 9月製造業PMI升至50.1%，較前月回升0.3個百分點，重返榮枯線以上，顯示
製造業景氣有所改善 ，主要受生產活動回暖及價格上漲帶動 ，中小企業景氣同步改善 ，但仍低於榮枯線，大型企業表現相對
較佳，高技術製造、裝備製造維持擴張 ，消費品PMI升至50.7%，重回景氣區間 ，另一方面，原材料及能源價格上漲推升企
業成本，上下游獲利差距持續擴大 。 非製造業PMI則由49.0%升至50.2%，服務業受節慶效應帶動回溫 ，建築業則受財政資

金投入，及基建建設推進而改善，不過需求復甦仍偏溫和，企業成本壓力尚未明顯減輕。
• 結論：第四季隨著基建投資、財政支出及房市支持政策逐步落地，經濟可望獲得支撐，但10月受長假因素影響，PMI可能短
期承壓。
資料來源： 萬聯證券，2026.9.30資料來源： 萬聯證券，2026.9.30

台灣景氣燈號連9紅 台股Q4有望突破5萬點大關
• 8月景氣對策燈號續亮紅燈： 綜合判斷分數41分，較前月持平，對應的燈號仍是代表景氣熱絡的紅燈 ，連續第9個月亮出紅
燈，追平史上最長「連紅」紀錄，顯示國內經濟維持成長。國發會表示，AI需求令台廠積極擴廠，加上政府推動AI新十大建
設，民間投資有望維持動能。此外，受惠財富效應、企業調薪及就業市場穩定，民間消費有望持續成長。
• 法人看好台股年底前站上5萬點：根據歷史統計第四季台股上漲的機率接近八成 ，除了AI投資趨勢未減，年底集團作帳行情
以及美國11月期中選後不確定性下降有望支撐台股多頭情緒 。群益投顧樂觀看待台股第四季表現 ，有望上看53,000點。此
外，彭博綜合統計台灣加權指數12個月目標價達59,688點。
• 觀點：近期台股站上48,000點整數關卡，總市值達5.3兆美元，讓台股耀升成為全球第四大市值股市 ，顯示台股在AI浪潮下

的得天獨厚。年底前仍須留意中東衝突進展，若能源通膨壓力持續，聯準會升息預期恐為股市帶來波動，但考慮AI科技持續
發酵，建議投資人續抱台股，並以定期不定額的策略紀律加碼，目標追求中長期完整的AI趨勢回報。
資料來源：國發會，2026.09.29。

外匯對沖視角—美債對沖後殖利率屬全球最低之一
• 美國國債殖利率真的高嗎？在當前全球總體經濟環境下，美國國債殖利率雖維持在數十年歷史高點附近，但若單憑名目殖利
率來判斷美債的吸引力，容易忽視匯率對沖與各國財政風險帶來的溢價差異。
• 美元視角下的殖利率比較： 若將其他已開發市場的主權債務 (如德債、日債、英債)完全對沖回美元，美債在殖利率曲線上多
數期限的實際殖利率，其實與德債並列全球最低水準。相反地，日本國債與英國國債經匯率對沖回美元後的收益率反而更高。
• 跨貨幣資產交換 (ASW)的真實回報： 以10年期主權債的對沖跨貨幣資產交換計算：美國 10年期國債有效殖利率約 4.85%，
日本10年期國債(對沖至美元)有效殖利率約5.06%，德國10年期國債(對沖至美元)有效殖利率約4.51%，這表明美國政府發

債的真實對沖融資成本其實已相對精實，且符合國際市場對各國財政基本面的合理定價。
對沖至美元後的10年期主權債殖利率

財政基本面與信用風險—殖利率溢價反映國家債務比重
• 從歐元計價或信用風險的角度來比較 ，美債的定價邏輯非常符合總體經濟基本面： 儘管債務以歐元計價時 ，10年期美債殖
利率比同期限德國國債高出約30個基點，這反映出德國的債務佔GDP比重僅約65%，而美國則高達127%，因此美債承擔了
適度的財政溢價。儘管義大利的債務佔GDP比重高達140%且信用前景較遜色，但在統一貨幣基準下，義大利國債殖利率仍
較美國國債高出60個基點，這顯示市場並未過度壓低美債信用，美債依然享有優質避險資產的溢價優勢。
• 海外私人投資者的資金流向：近一年海外私人機構在美債加碼速度放緩，乃通膨環境下全球其他資產選擇增加，及過去升息
波段導致帳面按市值計價虧損 。然而歐洲投資人(持有一半以上的海外美債)目前仍持有龐大部位，並在等待適當的加碼時機。

• 小結與投資建議：在經過外匯對沖後，美債收益率在發達國家主權債中處於相對低位，發行成本合理且具競爭力。美債殖利
率高於德債低於義債 ，精確地反映美國政府債務負擔率 (127%)與總體經濟穩健度 。在全球公債遭遇拋售潮與通膨陰影下 ，
美債因利率期間結構與匯率優勢 ，今年以美元計價總回報優於多數 G7主權債，儘管海外私人機構投資人正在等待長端利率
落底訊號以重返市場，對美元資金或以美元計價為主的投資人而言，當前美債名目殖利率仍處於數十年高位，具備相當良好
的長期鎖利價值，但價格波動風險考量下，建議債券佈局仍以中短天期為主。
歐元計價美債殖利率高於德但低於義

亞洲晶⽚債券跑輸指數，評級保持穩健
• 亞洲晶⽚債券跑輸指數，評級保持穩健：儘管AI熱潮推動收⼊創下紀錄 ，但SK海力⼠和台積電的美元債 ，今年以來的表現仍
略遜於彭博亞洲美元信用債總回報指數 ，主因為大規模資本支出擴張 ，以及潛在的投⼊資本回報率下降令 其利差承壓，美國
雲服務巨頭創紀錄的 AI支出計畫加劇了壓力 ，儘管如此 SK 海力⼠和台積電仍保持較強的高評級信用實力 ，台積電計畫投
⼊600-640億美元資本支出 ，SK海力⼠的資本支出則處於 40兆韓圜區間。兩家公司穩健的資產負債表 、合計共1,260億美元
的淨現金、強勁的經營性現金流，以及在先進製程，和HBM晶⽚領域的市場領先地位，為上述支出提供支撐。
• 投資建議：現階段台積電與SK海力⼠美元債最大的問題不是信用風險 ，而是估值與利差壓力 ，短期可能因AI資本支出過大，

及市場情緒波動而表現落後指數 ，但以兩家公司強大的現金流 、淨現金部位及產業領導地位來看 ，信用品質仍屬亞洲投資級
債中的優質標的，建議利差擴大時分批布局 。

顧 • 企業獲利成長將支撐股價上行：標普500指數的長期走勢與企業每股盈餘（EPS）高度連動。高盛預估，標普500企業EPS將
於2026年達375美元，並在2027年進一步升至415美元，對應年增率約36%與11%。在利率維持高檔、金融條件偏緊的環境
下，企業獲利仍持續處於成長軌道，將為股市提供穩健的中期上行動能。
• 本益比回落提供潛在布局契機：根據FactSet數據，標普500企業第三季整體獲利預計成長30.6%。其中，資訊科技類股獲利
成長預估高達66.5%，領先能源以外的其他產業；同時，該類股預估本益比已由今年高點回落，在獲利上修與估值修正同步
發生的背景下，浮現具吸引力的中期布局機會。
• 小結：短期市場仍可能受地緣政治與利率預期擾動，但中期趨勢仍由企業獲利擴張所主導。建議投資人採取分批布局科技股

策略，聚焦具備AI受益結構、資本支出能見度高及現金流穩健的龍頭企業。

2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

## Document 064: 2026年金融街论坛学习体会：货币政策传导效率进一步提升

### Page 1

证券投资咨询业务证书编号：S1300516090001

2026年金融街论坛学习体会

货币政策传导效率进一步提升   
主要政策利率即将换锚 。贷款利率定价基准趋向多元化。进一步出台稳市举
措。支持 AI 发展，推进 AI 应用。 
 10 月 19 日，2026 金融街论坛年会正式开幕，年会主题为“开放・合作・
包容——共筑全球金融发展新生态”。 
 主要政策利率 即将换锚。中国人民银行行长潘功胜发表题为《 关于当前
市场关注若干问题的思考 》的主题演讲。潘行长 在演讲中指出，DR001
“作为货币市场交易活跃、最具基准性的利率，能够灵敏反映短期资金
供求状况”，已成为央行货币政策调控的目标利率。 
考虑到当前央行公开市场操作的主要品种及利率期限均仍为 7 天，潘行
长指出，央行“将研究择机把央行公开市场主要操作品种的期限切换为
隔夜，公开市场隔夜回购利率将相应成为主要政策利率 ”。此次调整将
主要政策利率与目标利率 的期限对齐（均为隔夜） ，将央行日常流动性
管理精准至 单日，短端利率调控机制 得到有效优化， 有助于提升利率传
导效率。 
从我国利率走廊建设的角度看，2024 年央行创设临时隔夜正/逆回购工具，
利率为 7 天逆回购利率−20bp / +50bp，作为我国利率走廊边界。2026 年 6
月央行进一步优化利率走廊，将临时隔夜正/逆回购工具利率调整为 7 天
逆回购利率−20bp / + 20bp，但利率走廊操作基准利率仍为 7 天逆回购利
率。央行此次将公开市场主要操作品种期限切换 至隔夜，完成了基准利
率、走廊边界及目标利率期限 的对齐，有望进一步提升我国流动性管理
政策的精准度，平缓未来利率波动。 
 贷款利率定价基准 趋向多元化。潘行长在讲话中指出， 未来“财务能力
强的企业”可在 DR001 和 LPR 之间自主选择浮动利率贷款定价基准，LPR
不再是贷款 利率的单一基准，一定程度上赋予了企业、银行 在信贷活动
中定价基准的选择权。这一举措或能在 疏通央行政策传导渠道的同时，
降低实体融资成本。当前我国 LPR 报价按月公布，DR001 则为日频成交
价格，后者对银行间资金成本反映更加充分；以 DR001 为贷款利率定价
基准，或能 使贷款价格与银行间流动性 水平进一步对齐 ，在银行间流动
性宽松的情况下，推动“财务能力强的企业”融资成本下降。 
 进一步出台 稳市举措。潘行长在讲话中 提出“设立应对非银机构大额赎
回的流动性支持工具 ”，旨在进一步 建立债券市场稳定机制。此外，证
监会主席吴清也在讲话中提出 “加强风险研判和政策工具储备，巩固完
善稳市机制建设 ”，“扩大中长期资金来源、渠道和入市方式 ”，“壮
大市场稳定力量”。 
 支持 AI 发展，推进 AI 应用。潘行长指出央行要做好三方面工作：“密
切跟踪研判人工智能发展的宏观影响 ”；“做好金融支持人工智能产业
发展工作”；“稳妥推进人工智能在金融领域的应用” ； 并特别提出“完
善人工智能企业融资支持体系 ”，金融对科技创新的支持强度有望进一
步提升。在推进 AI 应用方面，吴清主席也在讲话中指出将“发布实施人
工智能在证券期货业安全合规应用的意见”，并促进 AI“在风控、投研
等重点场景安全高效应用”。 
 风险提示：海外大宗商品价格震荡；地缘关系的不确定性；政策效果不
及预期。

评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

AirTAC International  | Asia Pacific
September Sales Rose 3%
MoM, 27% YoY
Here we focus on Airtac's monthly sales, which we believe could
be a catalyst for its share price.
What's new:
• September sales came in at NT$3,811mn (+3% MoM/+27% YoY).
• 3Q26 revenue was NT$11,512mn (-5% QoQ/+40% YoY), in line with our
estimate of NT$11,586mn (-5% QoQ/+41% YoY) but slightly better than
consensus at NT$11,215mn (-8% QoQ/+36% YoY).
• By segment, shipments of machine tool (+35% YoY), energy & lighting (+30%

YoY), electronics (+27% YoY) and textiles (+25% YoY) outperformed during
the month.
Outlook:
• Both order booking and shipments continued to exceed company
expectations in the month.
• Looking ahead, management remains positive on its growth outlook, driven
by share gains and stimulus policies, which could help sustain the upcycle at
least into 2027.
• We remain OW as current valuation at 18x 2027e P/E is undemanding vs. its
mid-cycle average of 25x, though we think Hiwin (2049.TW) might

outperform on share price on the back of sequential growth in the top line
and potentially GM in 3Q26.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 29x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). As the industry enters an up-cycle, we think applying a near-peak cycle
valuation of 29x P/E is reasonable given the stronger business outlook.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China

n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation
Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple

of 38x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 47% operating profit
CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup.
n Market share gains.
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids.
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand.
n More pricing pressure.
n Market share loss.
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

研 究  報 告                
貿聯-KY(3665.TT) 
產業：電子–其他電子 
正式合併 Interplex 財報，大幅挹注 HPC 產品營收 
 投資評價與建議 
正式併購Interplex，預估2026/2027年稅後EPS為70.58/128.86元，過往貿聯
本益比位於17~30倍區間，考量併購與光通訊專案逐步發酵，給予25倍PER。 
 重點評論分析 
正式併購Interplex集團Datacom業務，大幅挹注HPC營收：近期貿聯正式合
併Interplex集團資料通訊業務，並自2026年9月23日起納入合併報表。  
Interplex產品包含：伺服器機殼、機櫃、滑軌與CPU Socket等，併購有利提
升貿聯在單⼀機櫃之產值，另外有利切入過往較難打進供應鏈之CSP客戶。 
HPC和半導體設備仍為主要獲利引擎 ， 光通訊佈局逐步發酵 ：展望2026~2027
年 ， 營收動能主要來自 ： (1)Ethernet AEC規格逐漸從400G往800G迭代 ， 800G 
AEC主要採用客戶為xAI與AWS，1.6T AEC預計將於4Q26隨Nvidia Rubin新
平台開始正式出貨，ALC主動式光纜目前已有2個以上客戶表示願意採用，預
估2H27開始貢獻營收 ； (2)Vera Rubin機櫃功率突破300kW ， 帶動Power Whip
規格從60A提升至100A 、 機櫃內Busbar規格從1,400A提升至5,000Ａ ， 電源規
格提升帶動產品單價隨安培數增加而提升；(3)Rubin Ultra機櫃Power Rack設
計帶動Power Whip用量與ASP皆顯著增加 ， 預估產值成⻑2倍以上 ； (4)貿聯與
ams OSRAM合作開發microVCSEL互連方案，另外與SENKO和ficonTEC合作
全自動化光學互連與光纖製備流程，目前光通訊營收已佔整體營收約2~3%。 
 投資風險 
伺服器新平台出貨進度不如預期、原物料成本上漲。

### Page 2

2

| 營運概況  
2Q26毛利率重回成⻑軌道，HPC與半導體設備需求持續強勁，3Q26營收創高：2Q26營收季增11.6%至232.6億元，係
因HPC與半導體設備業務需求持續強勁，1Q26毛利率僅28.8%，主要受車用等非AI低毛利產品復甦導致產品組合改變
以及xAI因併購使其資料中心建置進度停滯 ， 導致延後拉貨AEC與power產品 ， 貿聯產能利用率下降亦影響毛利率 ， 2Q26
毛利率季增1.96ppts至30.7%，重回過往水準，係因客戶逐步恢復拉貨，稅後EPS為15.28元。貿聯8月營收月減7.7%至
88.9億元，係因歐洲地區歷經季節性暑期⻑假，9月營收月增5%至93.3億元，營收達成率101%，符合預期，係因xAI逐
步恢復拉貨以及AWS Trainium 3放量，3Q26營收季增19.8%至278.9億元，預估稅後EPS為19.12元。 
正式併購Interplex集團Datacom業務，大幅挹注HPC營收： 貿聯-KY於2026年6月宣布斥資8.5億美元收購新加坡
Interplex集團資料通訊業務，近期正式合併並自2026年9月23日起納入合併報表。Interplex營運總部位於新加坡，產能
遍及南亞與亞洲地區，包括：泰國、越南、⾺來⻄亞與中國等，其核心技術為精密金屬加工與機構件，主要產品包含：
伺服器機殼、機櫃、滑軌與CPU Socket等，另外僅Busbar產品線與貿聯重疊且同為Nvidia MGX合格供應商，併購除擴
張HPC營收規模外，亦提升貿聯在單⼀AI Server機櫃之產值，另外透過併購有利切入過往較難打進供應鏈之CSP客戶。
2025年Interplex營收約4億美元，佔貿聯2025年營收之17%，隨AI運算與推論需求增加，假設Interplex 2026和2027年
營收皆年增30%，預估可貢獻貿聯2026/2027年營收分別約39與200億元。 
HPC 和半導體設備仍為主要獲利引擎，光通訊佈局逐步發酵，成為「銅光並進」主要受惠者：展望 2026~2027 年，貿
聯營收動能主要來自：(1)隨 AI 頻寬需求增加，Ethernet AEC 規格逐漸從 400G 往 800G 迭代，預計 2026 年底前成為
主流規格，800G AEC 主要採用客戶為 xAI 與 AWS，其中 xAI 因 AEC 採用規格最高且⻑度最⻑，故 ASP 較高，1.6T 
AEC 預計將於 4Q26 隨 Nvidia Rubin 新平台開始正式出貨，由於 1.6T AEC 採用 8 條單通道 200G 之線材組裝，故組
裝難度提升且成本翻倍，因此 ASP 和毛利率皆優於過往規格，另外 PCIe AEC 則已開發完成，將隨 CSP 推出 PCIe 版本
Switch 放量，ALC 主動式光纜目前已有 2 個以上客戶表示願意採用，預估 2H27 開始貢獻營收；(2)隨 Vera Rubin 機櫃
功率突破 300kW，帶動 Power Whip 規格從 60A 提升至 100A、機櫃內 Busbar 規格從 1,400A 提升至 5,000Ａ，電源
規格提升帶動產品單價隨安培數增加而提升；(3)Rubin Ultra 機櫃可容納 576 顆 GPU 使單⼀機櫃功耗超過 500kW．由
於機櫃無法容納電源設備，故單獨拉出成為 Power Rack．帶動「Power Rack 至運算機櫃」電源連接需求大幅增加，預
估 Power Whip 之用量與 ASP 皆顯著增加 ， 預估產值成⻑超過 2 倍以上 ； (4)光通訊領域方面 ， 近期貿聯與 ams OSRAM
合作開發 AI scale-up microVCSEL 互連方案，另外持續與 SENKO 和 ficonTEC 合作全自動化的光學互連與光纖製備流
程，目前光通訊營收已佔公司整體營收約 2~3%，顯示光通訊產品成⻑⼒道強勁。綜上所述，預估 2026/2027 年營收
年增 49.5%/55.4%至 1,064.9/1,654.6 億元，稅後 EPS 為 70.58/128.86 元。 
季度主要財務數據及預估值

## Document 199: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점불확실성보다 성장_3801b631

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점삼성바이오로직스(207940) 불확실성보다 성장을 볼 시점불확실성보다 성장_3801b631.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
3분기 실적 컨센서스 상회 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 1조 4,903억 원(+18.5% Y oY), 
영업이익은 6,401억 원(+1.0% Y oY, 영업이익률 43.0%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 각각 
10%, 5% 상회했다. 1~4공장의 풀가동이 이어진 가운데 5공장의 인증용 생산배치 
물량과 미국 락빌 공장 매출(약 1,000억 원)이 처음으로 반영되며 외형 성장을 
견인했다. 매출원가율은 50.4%로 전년 동기(41.4%), 전분기(47.0%) 대비 
상승했는데, 락빌 공장과 5공장의 초기 물량 반영에 따른 것으로 판단한다. 향후 
물량 상승에 따라 매출원가율은 감소할 것으로 예상한다. 그럼에도 EBITDA 
마진율은 50.4%로 높은 수준을 유지하며 7,515억 원을 달성했다. 순이익은 원/달러 
환율 하락에 따른 외화환산손실이 반영되며 3,911억 원을 기록했다.  
환율 하락에도 가이던스 상향하며 성장 궤도 유지 
2026년 연간 매출액은 5조 6,133억 원(+23.2% Y oY), 영업이익은 2조 4,605억 
원(+18.9% YoY)으로 전망한다. 최근 원/달러 환율 하락에도 이번 실적발표에서 
상향 제시한 연간 매출 성장률 20% 이상은 충분히 달성 가능할 것으로 판단한다. 
4분기에는 락빌 공장의 매출 약 1,000억 원과 5공장 가동 확대에 더해, 5월 
파업으로 이연된 물량의 매출 인식이 성장에 기여할 전망이다.  
투자의견 매수 및 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
비만 치료제 CDMO 진출을 위한 폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 공개매수 응모율 
87.88%로 최소 조건을 충족해 11월 말 완료를 앞두고 있어 신규 성장 동력은 
확보된 것으로 판단한다. 노사 리스크도 법원의 쟁의행위 제한 확대에 이어 노조가 
2차 총파업을 잠정 연기하며 크게 완화됐다. 미국 관세 불확실성으로 부진했던 신규 
수주 모멘텀은 올해 연말부터 내년에 걸쳐 강화될 것으로 전망하며, 불확실성 
해소와 성장 기대감을 반영할 시점이라 판단한다.

동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일 
해당 사항 없음

제약업종

◼ 3Q26 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회 
◼ 4Q26 지연 물량 인식 및 신규 공장 기여로 실적 개선 전망 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용). 기
존 공장의 높은 가동률을 기반으로 5공장 매출 기여 확대, 미국 생산거점 
확보, 신규 모달리티 진출을 통한 중장기 성장 지속 전망.

3Q26 Review: 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회  
3Q26 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5% yoy, +12.8% qoq), 영업이익 6,401
억원(+1.1% yoy, +9.1% qoq), 영업이익률 42.9%(-7.5%p yoy)를 기록하며 
당사 추정치 및 시장 컨센서스 상회. 5월 1차 파업에 따른 매출 차질(약 
1,500억원) 에도 불구하고, 기존 1~4공장 Full 가동이 지속되며 견조한 실
적 달성. 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대와 및 미국 록빌 공장 매출 인식도 외
형 성장에 기여. 다만, 신규 생산시설 초기 가동비와 제품 믹스 영향으로 
영업이익률은 전년 동기 대비 하락.

4Q26 매출액 1조 5,764억원(+22.6% yoy), 영업이익 7,075억원
(+34.0% yoy) 전망  
4Q26에는 2분기 파업으로 지연된 생산물량의 순차적인 매출 인식과 미국 
록빌 공장 매출 기여, 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대로 실적 개선 예상, 동사
는 신규 생산시설의 매출 가시성 확보를 바탕으로 2026년 매출 성장률 
20%를 무난히 달성할 것으로 전망(연초 가이던스 15~20%).

신규 수주 회복과 포트폴리오 확장에 주목 
미국 의약품 관세정책 불확실성 완화로 지연됐던 수주 협상이 재개되며 신
규 수주 환경 개선 중. 3Q26 약 3억달러의 신규 수주를 확보했으며, 대형 
계약 협상도 구체화되고 있어 2027년 수주 회복 기대. 이는 5공장 가동률 
확대와 중장기 성장 가시성을 높이는 요인. 6공장은 ‘26년말~’27년초 착
공을 목표로 기존 증설 일정 유지. 
폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 1차 공개매수 응모율 87.88%를 기록하며 연내 
인수 완료 추진 중. 인수 완료 시 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업영역이 확대되며 
글로벌 생산거점 및 고객 기반 다변화 기대.

## Document 225: AI抛售潮引爆增长恐慌，美联储加息预期推迟至明年1月

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI抛售潮引爆增长恐慌，美联储加息预期推迟至明年1月.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⾼盛分析表⽰， AI 疲软重新定价了资本⽀出周期，减轻了潜在债券发⾏的压⼒， " 影响股票
的问题，正在帮助解决利率问题。 "
摘要：
AI 科技股的集中抛售、法国主权债务危机加剧与中东油价冲击三重压⼒同步引爆，美国国债市场
迎来三年来最猛烈的单⽇反弹，避险逻辑重回主导。
周四， 10 年期美债收益率单⽇下⾏ 19.3 个基点⾄ 4.952% ，重回 5% 下⽅，创 2023 年 3 ⽉硅⾕银⾏
危机以来最⼤单⽇跌幅。数据显⽰，过去⼗年仅有 15 个交易⽇出现过如此幅度的单⽇波动。与
此同时，市场对美联储加息的预期随之后移 ——联邦基⾦利率期货⽬前完全定价于明年 1 ⽉加息
25 个基点，此前市场预期的时间节点为 12 ⽉。
这轮⾏情的深层信号在于：油价飙升之际，美债同步被抢购，市场将⾼油价解读为增⻓威胁⽽
⾮通胀威胁。野村跨资产策略师 Charlie McElligott 在客⼾报告中写道， " 债券正重新发挥对冲功
能 " ， " 市场似乎正在实时重新定价 AI 重置带来的美国 ' 增⻓恐慌 ' 概率。 "

三重冲击同步引爆市场
本轮美债急涨由三条独⽴⻛险线索⼏乎同时触发。
AI 财报季表现不及市场隐性预期是⾸要导⽕索。 Lam Research 业绩强劲但未突破预期上限；特
斯拉将全年资本⽀出指引从逾 250 亿美元下调⾄约 200 亿美元； Meta 虽上调全年资本⽀出指引下
限⾄ 1350 亿美元，但这意味着仅第四季度单季⽀出即需达 490 亿⾄ 590 亿美元，市场对执⾏可⾏
性产⽣疑虑。巴克莱信⽤分析师 Andrew Keches 在财报后报告中指出，Meta 未来将同时依赖信
贷市场与股票市场， " 债券发⾏的绝对规模也可能上升 " ，这将在⼀段时间内压制其信⽤利差。
法国主权债务危机同步升温。法国 10 年期国债收益率盘中⼀度升⾄ 5.142% ，为 2002 年 6 ⽉以来
最⾼盘中⽔平；法德 10 年期利差扩⼤⾄逾 170 个基点，创 2011 年欧债危机以来最⾼；法国信⽤
违约互换突破 100 个基点，为 2012 年以来⾸次。法国 10 年期收益率⼀度⾼于意⼤利同期限收益

率约 50 个基点，属历史上从未出现的情形。 DWS 美洲固收主管 George Catrambone 表⽰，法国
的动荡 " 带来了⼀定的避险买盘 " 。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

中东局势同步加剧。胡塞武装对沙特阿美沙伊巴赫油⽥发动⽆⼈机袭击，布伦特原油重上每桶
112 美元， WTI 原油价格逼近每桶 100 美元。 PGIM Credit 全球债券主管 Robert Tipp 表⽰： " 中东
局势升级、法国问题持续恶化以及 AI 领域疑虑上升 —— 你看到的是多重压⼒同时爆发。正常交易
⽇，⼀次像样的波动也就⼏个基点。今天你看到的是三倍于此。 "
AI 资本⽀出叙事动摇，增⻓叙事转向
⾼盛策略师 Rich Privorotsky 将本轮⾏情定性为 " 令⼈瞩⽬的反⾝性 " ：AI 疲软重新定价了资本⽀
出周期，减轻了潜在债券发⾏的压⼒，并⽇益演变为⼀次增⻓冲击。 " 影响股票的问题，正在帮
助解决利率问题。 "
逻辑链条清晰 ——超⼤规模云计算企业债券发⾏规模已可与美国国债相提并论， AI 资本⽀出若
出现收缩，实质上等同于减少债券市场的潜在供给压⼒。
与此同时，数据中⼼信⽤违约互换利差重新飙升⾄历史新⾼，超⼤规模云计算企业的整体信⽤
⻛险持续攀升并向更⼴泛的信⽤市场蔓延 —— 欧元区⾼收益信⽤违约互换指数 iTraxx Europe
Crossover 升⾄ 329 个基点，为近七个⽉最⾼⽔平。
彭博宏观策略师 Michael Ball 指出，随着融资成本上升，⾃由现⾦流将成为科技股更重要的分化
指标。AI 交易的下⼀阶段， " 可能更多关乎谁能在⽆需向资本市场伸⼿的情况下持续投⼊，⽽⾮
谁能砸最多的钱。 "
Mackenzie Investments ⾸席固收策略师 Dustin Reid 表⽰： " 这种级别的波动，通常意味着某种
恐慌或危机正在发⽣。我们正处于⼀个围绕 AI 过度扩张的主题之中。 " 这触发了资⾦从股票向美
债与固收市场的轮转。

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -49.02 điểm tương ứng -2.66%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dệt may, Y tế và Bất động sản là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -4,944 tỷ đồng, 
tập trung ở các mã VPB, TCB và VHM; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở mức 1995 
tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại bán ròng -5,280 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua và đang 
duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế 3,630 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 208 hợp đồng nhưng đang 
duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,428 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam tiếp tục điều chỉnh tuần thứ hai liên tiếp sau nâng hạng, khi VN-Index lùi 
về đóng cửa 1.737 điểm — giảm khoảng 2% so với tuần trước và đang kiểm định lại vùng 
đáy cũ của tháng 8, quanh 1.720. Áp lực bán duy trì khi chỉ số giảm nhiều phiên liên tiếp, 
cho thấy hiệu ứng "mua kỳ vọng, bán thực tế" sau nâng hạng vẫn chưa kết thúc, trong bối 
cảnh dòng tiền thận trọng và thanh khoản ở mức thấp. Khối ngoại tiếp tục là lực cản 
chính khi bán ròng mạnh cả 5 phiên trong tuần, riêng phiên 2/10 bán ròng tới 3.361 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở PNJ và HDB. Áp lực bên ngoài vẫn hiện diện khi Fed duy trì lập trường 
diều hâu và giá dầu ở mức cao; trong một, hai tuần tới, mùa báo cáo KQKD sẽ bắt đầu có 
tác động, kỳ vọng theo chiều hướng tích cực, qua đó giúp nâng đỡ thị trường chung.

Source: Trading View, KB Securities Vietnam

Ngắn hạn 
(Dưới 3 tháng)

Trung hạn (Từ 3 đến 9 tháng)

Chỉ báo  Nhận định xu hướng Hành động 
Xanh đậm  Khả năng tăng điểm cao Mua mạnh 
Xanh nhạt  Khả năng tăng điểm vừa phải Mua từng phần 
Vàng  Xu hướng trung tính Mua/Bán trading 
Cam  Khả năng giảm điểm vừa phải Bán từng phần 
Đỏ  Khả năng giảm điểm cao Bán mạnh

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành cây nến giảm điểm tiêu cực trên khung tuần, xuyên thủng vùng hỗ trợ 
gần quanh 1750, cho thấy áp lực bán đã có phần quyết liệt hơn sau 2 tuần giằng co trước 
đó. Trên khung ngày, sau các phiên lình xình vào đầu tuần, chỉ số cũng dần chuyển sang 
diễn biến giảm điểm rõ nét hơn về cuối tuần, đồng thời cắt sâu xuống dưới SMA50, thể 
hiện áp lực phân phối đang có phần lấn át mặc dù chưa xảy ra hiện tượng bán tháo khi 
khối lượng khớp lệnh được giữ ở mức thấp. Diễn biến tính từ vùng đỉnh 1870 vào đầu tháng 
9 đang không mấy khả quan, tuy nhiên xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn, tính từ đáy 1640 vào cuối 
tháng 7, hiện vẫn đang được bảo lưu và VNINDEX được kỳ vọng sẽ có nhịp hồi phục trở lại 
khi về đến vùng hỗ trợ kế tiếp tại quanh 1710. 
Kháng cự xa: 1820 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1775 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1710 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1670 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

## Document 260: Cliff Notes_ mulling the minutes (2026-10-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ mulling the minutes (2026-10-24).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-24
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: mulling the minutes
By Sian Fenner, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
11:10 October 09 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
The only data release of note in Australia this week was the October Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment
Survey, which reported a 4.7% drop in the headline index. At 80.4, sentiment has tumbled back to
deeply pessimistic levels, similar to the post-pandemic cost-of-living shock when readings were among

the weakest in the survey’s more than fifty-year history. Indeed, inflation and interest rate pressures are
once again clearly front of mind for consumers, with both the view on ‘family finances vs a year ago’

### Page 2

and ‘family finance next 12 months’ falling 8.0% and 6.4% in the month, respectively. Moreover, the
RBA’s September rate hike looks to have had a significant impact – sentiment tanked 20% between
those surveyed before the RBA decision and those surveyed afterwards.

Views on the economic outlook were relatively more resilient, but they still weakened, with the one-year
and five-year ahead sub-indexes falling 1.6% and 0.4% respectively. Consumers are becoming more
‘on edge’ about job security, albeit not outright alarmed. The unemployment rate remains relatively low
versus history, although it has drifted up to a cycle high of 4.6% as labour supply continues to outstrip
demand. We expect it to hold there in next week’s September Labour Force Survey – see our preview
for more detail. All told, with higher interest rates, renewed inflation pressures and a softening jobs
market all weighing at once, households are entering the final months of the year on the back foot.

Offshore, the focus was on US data.

Before turning to this week's releases, it is worth noting that September non-farm payrolls came in
softer than expected across most metrics. Employment rose by only 29k (89k expected), and there
were sizeable downward revisions to prior months, with a combined 60k removed from July and August
payrolls, leaving gains of just 31k and 29k respectively. The details pointed to weaker hiring in leisure &
hospitality and education & health services industries, although construction and manufacturing
employment continued to benefit from ongoing data-centre-related investment. The unemployment rate
edged up to 4.2%, with softer employment occurring alongside a rise in labour force participation to
61.8%. Meanwhile wage growth moderated to 0.1%mth (3.0%yr). Taken together, the report suggests
labour demand is cooling, with the three-month average pace of job creation slowing to around 50k.
However, softer payroll gains are not necessarily indicative of labour market deterioration. Lower
immigration and ageing demographics have reduced the pace of employment growth needed to keep
the unemployment rate stable. As such, the report is more consistent with a labour market that is
gradually normalising than one entering a meaningful downturn.

The September FOMC minutes reinforced a distinctly hawkish message, with participants noting they
had "not seen sufficient progress on lowering inflation in recent months" and expressing concern that
inflation risks remained "skewed to the upside". Policymakers highlighted that both core services and
core goods inflation remained elevated, with some attributing persistent goods price strength to the
effects of the AI buildout despite the sector's traditional disinflationary role. There was also concern
that, after more than five years of inflation above target, elevated inflation could begin to influence
inflation expectations and wage- and price-setting behaviour. In contrast, labour market conditions
were generally viewed as stable and "close to maximum employment". While a majority noted the
labour market had strengthened somewhat recently, several participants highlighted unusually low
dynamism, characterised by low hiring, low layoffs, a low job-finding rate and elevated long-term
unemployment. Importantly, participants generally judged that aggregate wage growth was moderate
and "consistent with inflation moving toward 2 percent", with several explicitly noting that "the labor
market was not currently a source of inflationary pressures".

### Page 3

Related articles
A key focus was the interaction between AI investment, productive capacity and inflation. While
participants broadly expected AI-related investment to boost productivity growth and potential output
over time, several warned that "the AI buildout could cause aggregate demand to outpace aggregate
supply over the medium term, putting upward pressure on inflation". Consistent with this view, some
participants had raised their estimate of the neutral policy rate and several judged the current policy
stance to be "not restrictive or only mildly restrictive". Against this backdrop, all participants supported
the September rate increase, and most assessed that another increase in the federal funds rate would
likely be appropriate by year-end, although future decisions would remain data dependent.

The September ISM Services PMI released this week painted a similar picture of an economy that
remains resilient but is gradually losing momentum. The headline index eased to 54.9 from 55.4 in
August, with the decline driven by a sharp fall in business activity and a 9.4pt drop in new export
orders, which slipped into contractionary territory for the first time in eight months. Labour market
conditions improved modestly with the employment index moving to broadly neutral territory 50.1 after
two months below 50, supported by elevated backlogs and still-solid demand. Respondent
commentary illustrated the mixed nature of labour demand, with some firms reporting they were "filling
positions vacated due to promotions or retirements", while others cited "restructuring due to efficiencies
gained using AI tools". Cost pressures also remained elevated, with the prices paid index rising to 74.0,
its highest level since July 2022 and around 18pts above its historical average, while fuel costs, tariffs
and supply chain constraints remained the most frequently cited concerns. Overall, the report suggests
services activity remains consistent with ongoing expansion, albeit at a slower pace, while labour
demand and pricing pressures remain firm.

Overall, the data supports our view that the FOMC will move to a broadly neutral policy stance by the
end of the year with one more rate hike, although the case for a back-to-back move in October has
become less clear cut.

## Document 291: Fed builds credibility with hike; one more likely in Dec

Source PDF: all report pdf/Fed builds credibility with hike; one more likely in Dec.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Fed builds credibility with 
hike; one more likely in Dec 
Summary 
Event: The US Federal Reserve hiked its key interest rate by 
25bps to 3.75-4.0%, in a unanimous decision. This marks the 
first hike since 2023, reversing the easing cycle that had 
taken rates down from 4.25% in September 2025 to a trough 
of 3.75% by December 2025. The revised projections 
indicate the Fed expects to raise rates by an additional 25bps 
this year, hold rates in 2027, and cut 25bps in both 2028 and 
2029. It upgraded the long-run rate slightly to 3.2%.  
Market reaction: The US yield curve mildly flattened , while 
the S&P500 index fell 0.5%. The policy rate sensitive US 2 -
year government bond yield jumped 7 bps to 4. 74% as 
markets factored in  three more 25bps hikes by June 2027 . 
The US 10-year yield rose 2bps to 5.02%, its highest since 
2007, while the 30-year yield was little changed. The USD 
index (DXY) rose 0.6%, breaking past its 50- and 100-DMAs. 
Investment strategy: Today’s move realises what we had 
been flagging in recent weeks i.e. the rising risk of a Fed rate 
hike following Fed Chair Warsh’s Jackson Hole speech and 
recent economic data prints, namely: a pick-up in job creation 
and inflation over the summer and a sharp rebound in oil 
prices due to the re-escalation of the Middle East conflict.  
This hike helps the Fed rebuild credibility as an inflation 
fighter. Given the unanimous decision to hike rates this week, 
we believe there will likely be one more hike before the end 
of this year, and one more in H1 2027 as we expect robust 
growth amid strong AI investments and a tight labour market, 
keeping inflation elevated.  That scenario would still be less 
than the three more rate hikes money markets are pricing 
after the latest Fed move. We believe policy rates can return 
to the 4.0 –4.25% range by end-2027, with the possibility of 
cuts in H2 2027  as the impact of tariffs and high oil prices 
start to fade towards the second half of 2027.   
Today’s refreshed Fed policy rate path is mildly bullish for the 
USD index; so we revise up our 3 -month target to 100. We 
also revise up our 3-month 10-year yield target to 5.0-5.25%.  
 The USD rose, while stocks fell after the Fed hiked rates 
and signalled one more rate hike this year; ultra-long 
bonds rose slightly as the Fed rebuild its inflation-
fighting credentials 
Asset performance: 15-Sep close to 16-Sep close

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

The Fed slightly revised up its growth projections for 
2026-27 and inflation projection for 2026; however, it 
still sees inflation cooling over the next two years

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 17 September 2026

Backdrop to the rate hike 
In hiking rates, Fed Chair Warsh reaffirmed the inflation 
threat to the US economy. US headline inflation accelerated 
to 0.4% m/m in August and stayed elevated on an annualised 
basis at 3.4% y/y. Energy prices , driven by the Middle East 
conflict, were a primary contributor. Oil prices have extended 
gains since the August inflation report, surging past USD 
100/bbl. Meanwhile, core inflation rose 0.3% m/m in August, 
beating estimates for a 0.2% rise . Also, US producer prices 
jumped 0.4% m/m in August, the most since May, and 5.4%  
y/y, further reinforcing the inflationary backdrop. 
The inflation report follow ed a stronger -than-expected jobs 
report for August. US monthly job creation has surged to an 
average 80,000 this year, from 10,000, tightening a job 
market that already faces a supply crunch from the Trump 
administration’s clampdown against immigration.  The 
combination of an acceleration in job creation and inflation 
over the summer, combined with a rebound in oil prices, 
supported our expectation for a Fed rate hike this week.  
Removing “a dose of accommodation” 
Warsh framed the decision to hike as removing "a dose of 
accommodation", rather than the start of an aggressive rate 
hiking cycle, and we agree with that characterisation. The aim 
is to bring financial and credit conditions more in line with the 
Fed's price stability objectives. To quote Warsh: “Our 
predominant focus is on the price stability side of our 
mandate. The plain fact is that inflation is too high, and has 
been for too long….This  summer’s inflation readings do not 
tell me that underlying trends have meaningfully improved.” 
He added that many categories in the last consumer and 
producer inflation reports are still showing price increases 
above 3% on a six- and 12-month basis.  
The unanimous vote to hike rates, contrasts with a 9 -3 split 
vote in July to hold rates. When combined with the pencilling 
in of one more hike by December, this suggests a majority of 
Fed policymakers have turned more hawkish over the 
summer. In doing so, they have helped rebuild the Fed’s 
inflation-fighting credentials and its independence against 
President Trump’s pressures to cut rates. This explains why 
long-end bond yields were stable after this week’s hike. 
Nevertheless, Warsh provided little guidance on the future 
rate path, as he held what was reportedly the shortest post -
meeting press conference on record for a Fed chair . This is 
consistent with his stated preference for less central bank 
communication. We expect more clarity once the Fed task 
forces submit their reports by year end. 
— Jonathan Liang, CIO for Fixed Income and FX   
Rajat Bhattacharya, Senior Investment Strategist

投資焦點低波價值防禦
► 美、歐、日主要央行連袂升息 ，加上美伊衝
突惡化 ，國際油價高漲 ，通膨疑慮與成熟國
家債務壓力 ，使全球多數國家公債殖利率衝
高，市場擔憂高利率與高油價可能拖累經濟
成長，全球股債匯市承壓 、波動上升 ，並以
此前領漲多頭行情的晶片 、半導體和科技股
最明顯 。觀察今年以來 ，不止股市波動度上
升，債市的波動更不亞於 股票市場 ，統計過
去，當VIX恐慌指數與MOVE債券波動度走高
時，低波動或防禦股的表現 ，往往比成長股
更好，顯示當市場震盪加劇時 ，資金傾向轉
往穩健資產停泊。
► 最近一次聯準會升息 ，為2022 年因疫情爆
發、供應鏈中斷 ，導致通膨大幅上揚 ，迫使
央行採取緊縮貨幣政策 ，和目前通膨上升促
央行升息背景 類似。前次聯準會啟動升息後
數月，成長股 、費城半導體股表現受抑 ，防
禦股、價值股和低波動族群 ，在剛開始升息
的環境下 ，表現相對抗跌 。考量目前聯準 會
籠罩在升息陰霾下，可增加相關類股配置。
對等關稅宣布後，內需防禦股獲利下修幅度最少 最近一次聯準會升息後，防禦低波高息和價值股突出美國股債波動度上升時，低波動、防禦股表現較佳

金融市場波動上升，增配低波、防禦價值股更穩健

### Page 8

8
► 觀察最近一季 ，資訊科技 、通訊服務與非核
心消費等High Beta類股，表現明顯落後於大
盤；反觀價值股 、防禦股和低波動等因子 ，
甚至核 心消 費與 高 股利等 傳統類 防禦 性產
業，報酬相對較 穩健，受資金青睞 。顯示在
經歷過 上半 年科 技 、半導 體股的 大漲 行情
後，市場預期高 利率與地緣政治 風險可能維
持更久時間 ，重新聚焦在獲利持久、現金易
落袋為安的防禦型資產，更能持盈保泰。
► 從預估本 益比看，目前金融僅 16倍、防禦股
17.5倍、價值股 (以IWD ETF代表)17.9倍、
低波動 (以USMV ETF代表)19.6倍，皆低於
標普500指數 現在 的21.3倍，近月資金流向
物美價廉的低波動價值 、防禦族群，評價越
低、漲幅越高 。展望後市，AI長線基本面仍
持續看好 ，股市回檔或為評價 修正而 非盈餘
下滑，市場波動度提升之際 ，建議不過度 集
中於單一 產業或 個股，多元配置適度增加 低
波動 、 防禦 和價 值股，分 批布局 以控管 風

險，料將更能在震盪加劇時安穩求利。

### Page 9

9
股市
 美、歐、日央行升息抑通膨 ，利率創高添股市波動風險 ，加上成熟國家債務攀升 ，科技巨擘亦舉債
投入資本支出 ，未來獲利與變現 能力持續被市場檢視 ，金融市場震盪不減 。美伊衝突升溫，近期可
透過防禦、低波動或價值 股來降低風險；科技 及AI半導體等發展趨勢不變 ，獲利基本面佳 ，長線發
展可期，宜採逢回分批布局方式，分散國家與產業配置。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，全球利率彈升有利銀行股 ，歐、日銀行股基本面佳 ，歐洲國防軍工
仍具投資吸引力。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融等產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升，應避免超長天期債券投資 ，預期年底前美國 10年期公債利率將於 4.5%-5.0%
震盪，可逢利率走升時，以中短天期債券進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款
能力，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。

債種：中天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金融、
科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
 日本銀行9月升息後，年底前或明年初仍可能再度緊縮，日圓震盪加大偏升。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪加大偏升
商品
 沙烏地阿拉伯關閉輸油管線 ，加上荷姆茲海峽局勢緊張 ，能源供需失衡 ，油價年底前維持 偏高水位。
 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力 與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法
類別 市場看法 偏好資產
策略思維
策略思維 多元配置、低波價值防禦與成長兼備，分批布局以控風險

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

### Page 2

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?
Institutional adoption of stablecoins continues to incrementally progress in 2026. While
the frenzy from GENIUS has diminished, stablecoins’ links to the financial system have
continued to deepen, and more policymakers are seriously considering their use cases.
The market cap has hovered around ~$300bn over the past 12 months, a sign that the

stablecoin market is maturing. Stablecoin payments, while still marginal relative to
stablecoins' crypto-native purpose, are also growing, rising from around $103bn in
adjusted transaction volume in 2022 to $375bn in 2026 YTD.
For now, institutional adoption remains concentrated in cross-border payments, treasury
management, 24/7 liquidity provision, collateral, and real-time settlement.
The next big question is whether stablecoins will evolve to become merely a payments

technology, or a genuine disruptor to the global banking system. As we previously
established, the US promotion of stablecoins remains a geopolitical tool to ensure USD
dominance. Bloomberg also reported that the Trump administration is considering joint
ventures with private actors to promote USD stablecoins overseas, in a bid to boost the
dollar’s dominance and source demand for US Treasuries. Last week, Circle announced
plans to expand into 40 new markets in Southeast Asia, LatAm and the Middle East. 99%

of stablecoins (by market cap) remain pegged to USD and are largely backed by US
Treasuries.
Looking ahead, the key debate to watch is whether US regulations will allow for yield-
bearing stablecoins. Research shows that consumers say policymakers should prohibit
crypto companies from offering interest-like rewards for holding stablecoins, which will
threaten to siphon off the bank deposits that drive local lending. The US CLARITY Act
failed in Sep after lawmakers failed to agree on whether the broader crypto market

structure would support such yield-bearing stablecoins.
In this chartbook, we revisit the stablecoin market a year after the GENIUS Act was
signed. We look at: (i) how adoption has evolved, (ii) the emergence of stablecoins as
financial market plumbing via liquidity provisioning and collateral management, and (iii)
the key regulatory debates that will determine the industry’s next direction.
Payment volume has a +66% CAGR

### Page 3

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
REFRESHER: What are stablecoins?
– Definition: Digital tokens pegged to “stable” assets (e.g. USD or gold), issued
and transferred on a blockchain, which records ownership and transactions.
– Fiat-backed stablecoins are considered fully “collateralized”: USD
stablecoin with 1mn coins in circulation would be backed by $1mn in
cash and equivalents held in reserve by the stablecoin issuer.

– Types: Fiat-, commodity-, crypto- and algorithmic-backed.
– ~99% of stablecoins market valuation is pegged to the USD.
– Market cap: $5bn (2020) to ~$310bn (2026)
– Concentrated in USDT (~60%) and USDC (~23%).
– Benefits: 24/7 transfers & settlement, lower financial intermediaries,
programmability (e.g. escrow, conditional release, automated reconciliation),
and dollar-based store of value.
– Use cases:*
– Crypto trading (~77%) remains dominant.

– Payments (5-12%) growing, cross-border transfers and remittances.
– Store of value (17-33%) especially in emerging markets.
– Other: (~2%-8%): on/off ramps and tokenized RWA settlement.
– Alternatives: CBDCs, tokenized deposits, traditional financial architecture
*Estimates on market share taken from BCG analysis.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg Finance LP. Shows stablecoin total
market cap. Updated October 192026.
Stablecoins’ reached a plateau of ~$310bn in October 2026

### Page 4

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
ADOPTION OVERVIEW: Most stablecoin volume is still not driven by real economic
activity
Stablecoin transaction volume:
– ~$100trn (unadjusted), ~$2.42trn (adjusted) in 2026 YTD, according to
Allium.
– Chainalysis places more bullish estimates of $28trn in 2025; projected to
reach $719trn by 2035 on organic growth alone.
Stablecoin payments:
– Stablecoin payment volume grew from $151bn in 2022 to $375bn 2026 YTD

(according to VisaOnChain Analytics).
– Businesses account for the majority (58%-64%) of payments, led by B2B
payment volumes.
– Allium Sep report showed largest business payment categories include
service fees ($56bn), payroll ($43bn) and service payments ($32bn).
– Case study: Payments volume across Visa’s >160 stablecoin-linked card
programs across consumer, business and commercial activity grew ~200%
y/y.
Strongest adoption in remittances, B2B payments and dollar access in countries

with unstable currencies or weak banking systems.
– Benefits for cross-border use cases: Instant settlement, <$0.10 per
transaction, borderless, transparent, 24/7, no intermediaries. Remittance fees
are lower (0.5-3.0%) than the global fee average (~6.6%).
– Stablecoins represented 0.31% of fiat cross-border payment value in 2025.
Marginal portion but registered +64% growth increase vs fiat (+9%) according
to Allium. B2B payments had the highest cross-border share (43%).

– A 2025 IMF study also shows transaction flows reached  ~7.7% of GDP in
Latin America and ~6.7% in Africa and the Middle East.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium. Shows top 8 countries by received payments from Jan to August 2026.
Only the receiving end is attributed, so a $100 payment counts once, not twice. That attribution covers 13.5% of
stablecoin payments.

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
CONSUMER/RETAIL PAYMENT: Still on the periphery
Consumer payments: Not yet there
– Retail adoption is still the most distant use case, especially amongst developed-market consumer credit card spend. A recent ABA survey found that only
24% of consumers think that stablecoins could provide meaningful benefits.
– Stablecoins still look more like a debit instrument than a credit product. They do not naturally replicate the consumer valueproposition of credit cards (i.e.

rewards, revolving credit, dispute rights, chargebacks, fraud protection and convenience), so there is limited incentive for developed-market consumers to
use them.
– Limited scope for payments also evident in wallets (see chart below). Very little of the stablecoin supply is being held as balances in consumer wallets.
Still, the number of retail payments is incrementally growing, with cross-border, emerging market use cases strongest
– Retail transaction volume: $2.6bn (2020) to $71bn (2024); $66bn YTD in 2026.*

– Strongest use in: (i) economies with hyperinflation; (ii) poor banking access; or (iii) high remittance fees.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium data.  Shows circulating supply by holder type (Sep 2026). Categories calculated as a percentage of total circulating supply. Note that wallets account for money being held vs
payments (money moving). Business wallets in this case refer to those holding smaller balances with quick turnover (i.e. payment services, merchants, gaming), while institutional wallets hold larger balances and turn them

over quickly, i.e. financial-services firms, treasuries, and wallets. Note: *Retail sized transactions are defined as transactions of $250 or less, according to Visa OnChain Analytics.
The amount of stablecoin supply in consumer wallets stands at ~14%

### Page 6

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
CASH/LIQUDITY MANAGEMENT: Stablecoins are evolving as a major tokenized
contender
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, taken from DB’s Tokenised money and the future of FXand Stablecoins and
agentic commerce: new rails, old frictions.
Stablecoins can help treasury management and settlement
– Stablecoins have a strong use case for settling transactions for FX,

treasury operations and collateral management i.e. intra-day repos.
– This is because stablecoins’ 24/7 settlement can address
operational frictions in cross-border transactions.
Poses significant risk to cross-border B2B transactions for banks
– Compared to traditional banks, stablecoins offer substantial relief
from transaction costs. Our equity analysts estimate total customer
processing fee savings to increase from 0 to ~$70bn in 2035, if
consumers switch from traditional banks to stablecoins.

Stablecoins is not a replacement solution to FX settlement risk
– FX settlement requires two currencies to be exchanged
simultaneously. Currently, stablecoins only serve as an intermediary
between the two currency legs, resulting in a “stablecoin sandwich”.
– Overwhelming concentration of USD-stablecoins also limits
stablecoin ability to support widespread payment-versus payment
settlement across currency pairs.
– Stablecoins also face fragmented interoperability/liquidity issues

and regulatory uncertainty across issuers/networks/jurisdictions.
– For more, read our FX report here.
Should we explain how settlement work as it is very
interesting and Rohini has a nice chart on this ?
Probably more relevant than having the stablecoin
market cap here I would say.

### Page 7

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
INSTITUTIONAL ADOPTION: Corporates, card networks, and banks are integrating
stablecoins
Institutions are racing to capture stablecoin market share
– Card networks: Mastercard expanded its settlement capabilities to include
stablecoins, using USDC and Paxos-issued stablecoins.
– Institutions: Exploring stablecoins via developing in-house permissioned

blockchain experiments and/or exploring issuance of proprietary stablecoins.
– >140 companies and financial institutions helped launch Open USD
(OUSD) in Sep 2026. OUSD reached $799mn of supply in 2 weeks.
Signals shift toward consortium-backed/network-led digital dollar
models.
Are stablecoins posing a threat to traditional payment systems?
– Bullish: Stablecoins to surpass traditional rails by the 2030s (Chainalysis).
– ~17% of stablecoin-linked card volume is occurring across Visa’s

business and commercial card programs. Shows growing adoption of
stablecoins for settlement, treasury management and cross-border
commerce.
– Key concerns for banks: Fee pressure from stablecoin adoption →
bank outflows → higher reliance on wholesale funding → pressure on
net interest margins.
– Base case: However, Deutsche Bank equity analysts see more limited near-
term risk. Visa and Mastercard to remain dominant especially in advanced
markets.

– KYC, AML, sanctions screening, wallet monitoring, fraud controls and
jurisdiction-specific limits remain essential for banks.
Stablecoin transactions could match Visa and Mastercard transactions
sometime between 2031 and 2039 (Chainalysis).
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Chainalysis.
Shows projected years until crypto merchant transactions reach Visa scale.

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
GEOPOLITICS: Why stablecoins are a "Genius" idea for the US
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, US Treasury, Tether July 2026 report. Shows Major foreign holders of Treasury Securities.
Treasury data from June 2026.
Can sustain USD dominance
– 99% of stablecoins (by market cap) are USD-pegged.
– Stablecoins function primarily as a digital version of dollars

moving over blockchain rails, extending the dollar’s reach into
new digital environments.
– Reinforces dollar savings: The more firms invoice and transact in USD,
the more they save in USD assets. Stablecoins help preserve this
feedback loop by keeping businesses and individuals in dollar
payment rails.
Can support US debt, given share held by foreigners has fallen from
>50% in early 2000s to ~30% in early 2026.
– Stablecoin issuers total holdings: ~$170bn according to Allium x

Citizen.
– Tether: ~$141bn in direct and indirect exposure to US T-bills.
– Impact: BIS shows how $3.5bn inflow into dollar-backed stablecoins
lowers 3m T-bill yields by 2.5-3.5bp under normal conditions. Effect
more than doubles in periods of T-bill shortages.
– Forecasts: T-bills holding by stablecoin issuers could reach ~$1trn by
2028 (Treasury forecasts).
– Recent Fed SF research places it closer to $400bn by the end
of 2030, lower than the US government’s financing needs.

Stablecoin issuers’  demand for US debt could counterweight declining demand from China
Tether competes with Saudi Arabia and South Korea as major holders of US Treasuries
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco, US Treasury Data. Shows Treasury
holdings, stablecoin issuers vs China.

### Page 10

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
US GENIUS ACT: Debate on rules around payment stablecoins one year on
GENIUS provisions
– Strict issuer requirements: Issuers can be private, non-bank entity, but must
meet strict federal or state requirements.
– Reserves: Requires 1:1 backing with US cash, 93 days T-bills, repos/reverse
repos, or money market funds holding those assets.
– AML/KYC/BSA compliance obligations to stablecoin issuers, aligning them

exams, and strong internal controls.
Next steps
– GENIUS takes effect no later than 18 Jan 2027, or 120 days after final
implementing regulations are issued.
– Ongoing pushback from US banks as to whether GENIUS ban on yield-
bearing stablecoins should extend to affiliate or third-party arrangements,
given many users do not hold stablecoins directly with the issuer.
– CLARITY ACT failed in Sep because of disagreements on how
exchanges/broader crypto market structure should handle stablecoins.

– The US Treasury has proposed implementing rules for payment stablecoins.
There are two ways stablecoin yield can be paid; ongoing debate over the three-
party model
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Congressional Research Service.

RISKS: Still hotly discussed amongst policymakers and financial institutions
Bank liquidity risk
Loss of stable funding source for banks from deposit outflow (not offset by
partial flowback from stablecoin issuers’ reserve deposit)
System stability risk
Stablecoins can fail to maintain their $1 value during times of stress. Evidence shows
that USDC and Tether actually trade within 1% of their dollar value but prices can

diverge sharply during stress episodes.
Run on stablecoin issuer(s) in market stress / trust scenario triggering fire sales of
reserve assets and/or deposit withdrawals.
Banks
Users / Depositors
❑ Stablecoins held in private
wallet or via exchange
❑ Purchases of blockchain-
based assets
❑ Instant, secure and low-cost
(cross-border) payments
Stablecoin issuer
Provider(s) of
reserve asset
❑ For USD stablecoins like
USDT/USDC mainly short-

Charts of the week: Diverging volatility

Strong earnings should resolve the bond and equity market divergence; French political risks remain largely contained  
S&P500 volatility index (VIX); US bond volatility index (MOVE)*   10-year government bond yield spreads over German bonds

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *measures US government bond volatility

Editorial 
Will earnings resolve the bond-equity split?  
Strategy summary:  Bond and equity market volatility has 
sharply diverged. Elevated bond volatility and political risks in 
the US and Europe warrant near-term caution, prompting us to 
lock in tactical gains in semiconductors and Taiwan equities. 
Our quantitative stock-bond model turned negative for the first 
time since March 2025, signalling near-term equity volatility . 
We stay invested in a diversified allocation  slightly overweight 
equities and prefer to navigate the upcoming results and macro 
events before adding high-beta exposure within equities. 
Consensus estimates point to a c. 31% y/y rise in S&P500 Q3 
earnings, driven by technology -sector earnings growth of c. 
67%. Earnings have beaten lofty estimates in H1 as the AI 
build-out exceeds expectations. This favours tech -led equities 
in the medium term. In Europe, French political and fiscal risks 
cloud the EUR outlook, but regional bank equities remain 
attractive on robust profitability, capital strength and valuations. 
Divergence between equities and bonds.  Equity markets 
signal calm, with the S&P 500 index near record highs and its 
volatility index (VIX) low at 15, while the US government bond 
volatility index (MOVE) is close to its highest since March. Bond 
stress reflects two-decade-high long yields amid strong growth, 
oil close to USD 100/bbl fuelling near -term inflation, fiscal 
uncertainty and crowded investor short positioning vulnerable 
to abrupt unwinds. High bond volatility could spill into equities. 
Our quantitative stock-bond model turns negative. For the 
first time since March 2025, the model score has turned 
negative, driven by deteriorating equity market breadth and 
above-average risk aversion. History shows global equity 
volatility rises most in the month after the score dips to -1. 
However, equities still outperformed bonds over the following 
1-12 months, on average, despite higher market risk. 
Taking some chips off the table; locking in gains:  Rising 
bond volatility, the negative stock -bond model signal, weak 
equity breadth and approaching US mid-term elections warrant 
near-term caution. US small -caps remain about 9% below 
August’s all-time highs even as large-caps reach new records. 
The macro uncertainty prompted us to lock in gains in 
semiconductor and Taiwan equity opportunistic ideas. 
Strong Q3 earnings season likely to overcome near -term 
challenges. Strong US and Asian earnings should ultimately 
resolve the equity -bond divergence in favour of stocks. 
Consensus S&P 500 Q3 earnings growth has risen to 30 .6% 
y/y, while technology is tracking 66.5%, driven by the AI 
infrastructure buildout. AI investment plans, monetisation and 
funding needs are key metrics to watch. Record memory -chip 
profits and higher long -term targets from AI leaders support 
staying long technology-led risk assets and using near -term 
volatility to scale into preferred sectors. Also, US equities have 
historically performed strongly in the 12 months after mid -term 
elections, particularly when the opposition regains control of the 
House and the Senate – as seems increasingly likely. 
Medium-term constructive on equities. Beyond Q3 earnings, 
we expect US earnings to rise 15-20% next year. Almost half of 
the estimated USD 420 per share of S&P500 earnings in 2027 
should come from tech-related sectors: information technology, 
communication services and consumer discretionary. We also 
favour US, Europe and Japan financial sectors as solid growth 
and higher rates lift lending income, while the AI buildout boosts 
advisory fees from IPOs and M&A s. However, a nother 30-
50bps rise in long-term bond yields , without earnings growth 
upgrades, could be a  headwind for equities.  Next week’s US 
inflation data for September is the next catalyst for bond yields. 
French uncertainty challenges near -term EUR view; 
continue to see opportunities in Euro area bank equities.  
Rising French political and fiscal risks have largely remained 
confined to France, with its bond yield spread versus German 
peers surging to the widest since the 2011-12 Euro area crisis. 
Nevertheless, EUR/USD has fallen around 4% since mid -
August, and our three-month forecast of 1.15 is under review. 
Tighter financial conditions and political uncertainty in France 
and Spain raises the risk of a n ECB rate hold. Euro area bank 
equities remain attractive on profitability, capital strength and 
valuations, subject to sovereign-spread and funding-cost risks.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya and Sundeep Gantori

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the market implications of France's political and 
fiscal uncertainty?   
Our view: France’s political and fiscal uncertainty remain near-
term headwinds for European assets. We are placing our three-
month EUR/USD forecast (1.15) under review for a potential 
downward adjustment, while retaining our 12-month forecast at 
1.18 as broader Euro  area spillovers remain limited. Our base 
case remains one more ECB hike in December followed by a 
prolonged pause, although tighter financial conditions increase 
the risk of a delay. For equities, we retain Euro area banks as 
an Opportunistic idea, supported by strong profitability, capital 
buffers and reasonable valuations. For bank bonds, we prefer a 
subordinated capital structure to senior debt. 
Rationale: France’s 10-year government bond yield has risen amid 
renewed political and fiscal concerns. This has tightened financial 
conditions and added to the near-term EUR risk premium. Together 
with elevated US yields, this raises downside risks to our three-
month EUR/USD view. However, the stress has not yet developed 
into broad Euro area contagion, and recent expectations of progress 
on France’s 2027 budget have provided some relief. Broader Euro  
area growth and labour  market fundamentals also remain relatively 
resilient, supporting our unchanged 12-month EUR/USD view. 
For the ECB, higher sovereign yields are already delivering some 
monetary tightening. Markets have consequently pared expectations 
of additional ECB hikes. We continue to expect one more hike in 
December, given elevated near -term inflation, but persistent fiscal 
or political stress in France and Spain could delay further tightening. 
For Euro area banks, wider sovereign spreads  are likely to  raise 
funding costs and pressure bond portfolios, while fewer ECB hikes 
would moderate further gains in net interest income. Nevertheless, 
profitability remains strong, capital buffers are healthy and price-
to-book valuations remain reasonable, with returns on equity also 
improving. We, therefore, maintain Euro area banks as an 
Opportunistic idea , while monitoring sovereign spreads, funding 
costs and any deterioration in loan-loss provisions.  
On the bond side, European bank junior subordinated bond prices 
fell by an average of 3-5 cash points before rebounding, with French 
bank subordinated bonds underperforming those of other European 
peers. As fundamentals remain strong, we view pullbacks as 
opportunities to add and maintain our preference for a subordinated 
capital structure over senior debt for European bank bonds. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist  
 Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is the likelihood of elevated long -end government 
bond yields evolving into a systemic risk for the financial sector 
and public finances? 
Our view: We believe s ystemic risk remains a tail risk rather 
than a base case in the near term. The probability would rise 
materially if rollover stress, foreign demand withdrawal and 
private credit deterioration occur in combination. 
Rationale: Higher bond yields worsen fiscal deficits and drive higher 
issuance requirements, exerting further upward pressure on yields. 
The resulting tighter financial conditions weigh on economic growth. 
This feedback loop is most acute in Developed Markets (DMs) where 
debt burdens are already elevated. For instance, projected 2026 
debt-to-GDP ratios stand at 204% for Japan, 138% for Italy, 126% 
for the US, 118% for France and 104% for the UK. Countries with 
shorter average debt maturities face the sharpest near-term 
repricing risk  as maturing obligations are refinanced at current 
rates. Meanwhile, evidence suggests that  a positive equity-bond 
correlation increases the sensitivity of yields to bond supply.  
Taking the US as an example, there has been no buyers’ strike on 
US government bonds yet , though foreign demand has softened, 
narrowing the margin of safety. The US government has shifted 
towards shorter-dated issuance to manage funding costs (reducing 
near-term expenses, but concentrating rollover risk). Economic 
resilience remains evident, underpinned by corporate confidence 
in AI -driven investment . The key question is whether this 
expansion can persist long enough to stabilise the fiscal trajectory. 
Within the financial sector, corporate balance sheets  remain 
strong, while High-Yield (HY) bond and leveraged loan default rates 
remain contained, though this reflects the lag between rate rises and 
credit deterioration, not immunity. Following the failure of the Silicon 
Valley Bank, liquidity discipline among banks has improved, but 
duration mismatches persist.  
In private credit, default rates are trending upward while the asset 
class has come under continued scrutiny , with negative headlines 
and a rise in redemptions trigger ing fund gating  at some  private 
credit managers since Q 1 2026. Hence, continued monitoring of 
default rates, redemptions and potential stress events in private 
credit remains essential . That said, higher rates could buoy 
medium-term returns and distribution yields in the asset class , 
as the majority of direct lending loans have a floating-rate structure. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

Most G8 economies have government debt-to-GDP 
ratios exceeding 100% 
Projected government debt-to-GDP ratios, 2026

Source: IMF, Standard Chartered

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is your outlook for the US Q3 2026 earnings season, 
especially for the banking sector? 
Our view: We expect another quarter of robust US earnings 
growth to support our Overweight stance on US equities. We 
see m ajor US banks, which begin reporting next week, also 
delivering healthy results, reinforcing our Overweight view on  
the US financial sector. 
Rationale: Consensus forecasts S&P500 Q3 earnings growth  of 
30.6% y/y, up from 27.6% at the start of July. Energy is expected to 
post the strongest growth at 114.7%, supported by elevated oil 
prices amid ongoing geopolitical tensions. Information technology 
(66.5%) and communication services (49.0%) remain key 
earnings drivers, benefiting from sustained AI -related demand and 
investment. The broader outlook remains supportive, with full-year 
earnings growth projected at 35.8% in 2026 and 15.6% in 2027. 
For banks, the recent underperformance amid concerns over higher 
long-term bond yields has, in our view, improved the forward-looking 
risk-reward profile.  Over the medium term, strengthening loan 
growth and resilient credit quality should continue to support solid 
fundamentals. Capital markets activity also remains constructive, 
with industry leaders well -positioned to benefit from their strong 
investment banking and trading franchises. 
—  Jason Wong, Senior Equity Analyst 
  How could upcoming semiconductor manufacturing and 
equipment earnings reshape the AI capex outlook? 
Our view: We maintain our AI capex outlook as robust demand 
and ongoing supply constraints support a multi-year expansion 
cycle. In the near term, however, we take a more prudent stance 
on semiconductors amid an expected rise in equity volatility. 
Rationale: Recent memory industry guidance points to sustained 
investment in advanced chip manufacturing , with customer 
commitments extending through 2031. Upcoming earnings should 
provide visibility into capacity expansion plans. This supports our AI 
capex outlook, which we will monitor through the earnings season.  
That said, we expect equity volatility to rise in the near term amid 
elevated long-term yields and macroeconomic uncertainty. Recent 
media reports on AI lab revenues  have also led to doubts over 
monetisation and the ability to sustain elevated capex spending. We 
thus took profit on our Global Semiconductors (8.1% return from 9-
Sep-26 to 2-Oct-26) and MSCI Taiwan (10.0% return from 9-Jul-26 to 
2-Oct-26) Opportunistic ideas following strong performance. This does 
not reflect a deterioration in fundamentals, but rather a need to be 
prudent on high-beta exposure. We remain constructive on the AI 
theme over the long term. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  USD/CHF has rebounded sharply over the past two 
months. What are the key factors behind the move, and do you 
see the currency pair continuing to move higher? 
Our view:  USD/CHF tested 0.84 amid strong momentum from 
the USD, but we see the pair remain ing supported near -term 
before gradually moving lower towards 0.82 in three months. 
Rationale: The Fed’s relatively high policy rate continues to support 
USD carry demand , while the Swiss National Bank maintains its 
policy rate at 0%. In the near term, however, the Swiss franc (CHF) 
could benefit from increased safe-haven demand amid mounting 
fiscal concerns in France  and political uncertainty in Spain , 
particularly if European risk premia continue to rise. Beyond these 
cyclical factors, Switzerland’s substantial net foreign asset position, 
persistent current account surpluses and low public debt burden 
continue to underpin the CHF’s structural strength . Over the 
medium term, we expect the USD’s rate advantage to gradually 
diminish as the Fed approaches the end of its tightening cycle, while 
the CHF’s defensive characteristics should remain supportive.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  Do you see further upside for USD/JPY after recent dovish 
comments from BoJ officials and latest bond auctions? 
Our view: We see some near-term upside risk to USD/JPY from 
elevated US  yields, but retain our medium -term constructive 
JPY view. USD/JPY remains below 159, suggesting markets are 
mindful of intervention risk as the pair approaches 160. We 
continue to expect further BoJ normalisation. 
Rationale: Recent BoJ communication appears less dovish than the 
market narrative had suggested. BoJ Governor Ueda continues to 
emphasise the need to anchor underlying inflation around 2%  
and gradually reduce monetary accommodation, supporting our view 
that the tightening cycle is not yet over. An October pause would 
reflect a more measured pace of policy normalisation  rather than a 
change in the BoJ’s policy direction. 
Recent Japanese government bond (JGB) auctions have shown 
resilient demand despite higher yields.  At the latest 10 -year JGB 
auction, the bid -to-cover ratio improved  to 3.76x from 3.29x 
previously, while the auction tail (spread between highest 
accepted yield and issued yield) narrowed to 0.2bps from 1.6bps. 
We see this as indicative of an orderly, functioning market rather than 
a dovish BoJ signal . Intervention concerns around  the 160 level 
should help limit near-term USD/JPY upside.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

are likely to be offset by CHF safe-haven demand

## Document 422: ‘Cost-Plus’ Costs (2026-10-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/‘Cost-Plus’ Costs (2026-10-23).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #labour #rba #productivity
‘Cost-Plus’ Costs
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
10:40 October 16 2026 Share
Entrenched expectations that cost increases can and should be passed on are precisely
what tight monetary policy is leaning against, but only indirectly.
The recent tendency of some firms to bundle into prices card-acceptance costs that had previously
been surcharged – and then some, in some cases – demonstrates a ‘cost-plus’ approach to pricing.

### Page 2

The recent banning of card surcharges has caused considerable upset this month, even though it is a
reversion to historical and international practice. The card schemes used to impose “no-surcharge”
rules, until the RBA banned these in 2003, and many other countries still have them. The turnaround
represents the RBA throwing in the towel on its longstanding effort to use price incentives to get people
to choose low-cost payment methods. Surcharges no longer made sense in a largely cashless
economy. In any case, least-cost routing puts the power to choose lower-cost methods in the hands of
the merchants who actually bear the cost.

In most cases, firms will simply roll the surcharge into the sticker price, leaving the total cost to the
consumer unchanged, just more transparent. We also understand that some large retailers have
absorbed the cost – if they were surcharging in the first place, which many did not. (The RBA estimated
that around 16% of businesses imposed surcharges.) Inevitably, though, some firms have lifted their
prices by more than any reasonable estimate of the merchant service fee that the surcharge was
meant to cover. And because this is Australia, of course takeaway coffee has been the centre of
consumer discontent.

Higher prices for one’s caffeine fix was unlikely to be the main instigator of this month’s slump in
consumer sentiment. Petrol prices are typically more salient for cost-of-living concerns, and the before-
and-after picture on the RBA announcement that week shows that higher interest rates also contributed
to the decline.

In some senses, though, the post-surcharge prices and the RBA’s decisions are linked: businesses
passing on increased costs into their own prices – and expecting to be able to make that increase stick
– is exactly the kind of inflationary environment the RBA is leaning against. Firms have been subjected
to an array of non-labour cost escalations, from pandemic-era supply chain issues to electricity to fuel
to water to building supplies. And IT equipment prices, which for decades were a reliable source of cost
reductions, are nowadays flat to up. Passing these costs on, leaving margins intact, demonstrates that
demand is not weak enough to prevent this, and signals that firms’ inflationary expectations might be
higher than the RBA desires. The RBA will take this as a sign that demand is still outstripping supply.

To the extent they can pass on these costs through to prices, it suggests that demand was strong
enough to bear it. The RBA will take this as a signal that demand is still outstripping supply.
This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. Base wages growth has been moderate for some
years, though the above-average increase in award wages this year will add a little. Not all firms will
seek to pass these cost increases on, but some will, including because they have limited scope to
absorb the cost increase into margins.
A cost-plus mentality has drawbacks, including that it limits the imagination away from finding
alternative, efficiency-improving responses. It also accords with recent academic research
suggesting that firms form expectations and set prices according to what is happening in their own
industry and experience, while putting little weight on economy-wide inflation or central bank
credibility. This is an unpleasant message for many consumers or anyone in an interest-sensitive
sector when supply shocks abound.

### Page 3

This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. While the Fair Work Commission did hand down an
above-average increase in award wages this year and this was probably passed through to prices in
hospitality and other labour-intensive sectors, base wages growth has been moderate for some years.
Business surveys point to non-labour cost escalation being the real issue lately. This is why we were
puzzled by the passage in the RBA minutes this week suggesting that cost pressures reported in
business surveys might imply that the unemployment rate consistent with inflation at target (the NAIRU)
has risen. It is a convenient belief, though, if unemployment is starting to overshoot your forecasts, as
the latest labour market data have done compared with the RBA’s August forecasts.

Not all firms can pass on cost increases. Most small firms do not have the market power, though coffee
may be an exception because convenience induces a kind of local market power. And as noted above,
large firms with everyday-low-price strategies will instead try to find savings elsewhere in the supply
chain.

Still, some firms will seek to pass these cost increases on. Some observers might see this as corporate
greed, but another way to frame this strategy is simply as a ‘cost-plus’ mentality. It turns out that cost-
plus pricing is especially common in areas where regulators determine the price – water and other
utilities being examples where costs and prices have escalated a lot in recent years.

There are limits to firms’ ability to absorb costs, too. The failures of so many building firms a few years
ago, squeezed between post-pandemic cost escalation, delivery delays and fixed-price contracts, were
a case in point. Normally, there is a contractual response, and it need not end in insolvency. The
episode does illustrate, however, that it is unrealistic to expect firms never to pass cost increases
through to prices.

The cost-plus mentality does have its drawbacks. It limits the imagination away from finding responses
that might save costs elsewhere to absorb the cost hike, and in so doing, boost efficiency. It was
equally limiting in times of soft demand: in the years before the pandemic, many firms professed a
belief that, because they couldn’t get price increases to stick, they could not possibly accept cost
increases, including in wages. Thus, Australia ended up with wages growth too low to be consistent
with inflation at target, and the peculiar spectacle of a central bank governor complaining that wages
growth was too low. And if labour remains cheap, why invest to economise on it and thereby boost
productivity?

A cost-plus mentality also amplifies the pass-through of supply shocks. This is not quite the same thing
as the ‘inflation expectations’ seen in standard economic models of inflation, but it is related. The
stronger the tendency towards cost-plus pricing, the worse the inflation response to a supply shock.
This is also true when inflation expectations are high, but the distinction matters. The recent academic
literature includes some fascinating empirical evidence from New Zealand that suggests that beliefs
about current and future overall inflation have little bearing on many firms’ pricing decisions. As related
work using French data shows, firms’ pricing choices are shaped less by economy-wide inflation
developments than by what is happening to their costs and in their industry.

### Page 4

Related articles
That adds up to economy-wide behaviour that looks a lot like firms (in aggregate) setting prices
according to expected inflation (in aggregate), but under the hood something quite different is going on.
If firms’ beliefs about future economy-wide inflation do not matter much in their pricing decisions, then
neither does the credibility of a central bank’s commitment to return inflation to target. The mechanism
to get inflation down then must be simply to weaken demand enough to convince firms that cost-plus
will not fly in the market. This is an unpleasant message for many consumers or anyone in an interest-
sensitive sector when supply shocks abound. It does, however, imply that if supply shocks fade or even
self-correct, so will high inflation. If and when that happens, tight monetary policy can be unwound.

The deeper question is whether cost-plus price dynamics can be reduced. That is more likely to be a
question for regulators than for central banks.
